**Title**  
**Crimson Vows**

---

### Blurb  

When the Italian‑American family that runs the “Borelli” underworld loses its matriarch, the power vacuum threatens to split the clan and unleash a war that could swallow every ally, lover, and rival.  

Evelyn “Evie” Caruso, a fiercely independent bartender with a reputation for being the only woman who can stand up to a gangster, finds herself pulled into the Borelli’s orbit when she refuses to sell her shop to the family’s treasurer. Her defiant courage catches the eye of the Borelli patriarch’s youngest son, Luca, a brooding heir who has sworn to protect his bloodline from the corrupt politicians who want to bring the family down.  

What begins as a tense business negotiation soon turns into a deadly dance of desire. Evie must navigate the treacherous waters of the Borelli’s loyalty codes, betrayals, and the ever‑looming threat of gunfire. And Luca, whose heart has been sealed by the trauma of his mother’s murder, finds a way to let his guard down only when she holds his heart in her hand.  

In a world where love can be as lethal as a bullet, Evie and Luca must decide whether the promise of a passionate romance is worth the blood that stains their hands.  

**Crimson Vows** is a dark, steamy saga that explores the price of love, power, and the relentless pull of family loyalty.

---

### Character List  

| # | Name | Role | Key Traits | Motivations |
|---|------|------|------------|-------------|
| 1 | **Evelyn “Evie” Caruso** | Protagonist, bartender & shopkeeper | Bold, empathetic, fiercely independent | Keep her shop safe, protect her community, discover why Luca is drawn to her |
| 2 | **Luca Borelli** | Male Lead, heir to Borelli family | Darkly handsome, protective, haunted | Preserve family honor, protect Evie, heal from his mother’s murder |
| 3 | **Marco Borelli** | Luca’s older brother | Ruthless, political, opportunistic | Expand the Borelli empire, manipulate the law to stay untouchable |
| 4 | **Nadia Borelli** | Luca’s mother (deceased) | Strong, motherly, principled | Keep family safe, keep her family together – a ghost that haunts Luca |
| 5 | **Salvatore “Sal” Rossi** | Loyal henchman, Luca’s right hand | Loyal, violent, street‑smart | Serve Luca, gain respect, find redemption |
| 6 | **Marta Rossi** | Sal’s wife, bartender | Warm, supportive, secretly ambitious | Secure Sal’s future, keep her family out of danger |
| 7 | **Gino “The Ghost” Santoro** | Rival gangster, Marco’s ally | Mysterious, ruthless, charismatic | Take over the Borelli empire, eliminate Luca’s family |
| 8 | **Detective Mara Quinn** | Police detective investigating Borelli | Tenacious, morally conflicted | Bring the Borelli down, protect innocent civilians |
| 9 | **Elena Caruso** | Evie’s older sister | Practical, protective | Keep Evie out of trouble, hold the family together |
| 10 | **Gianni “Gin” Moretti** | Ex‑lover of Evie, local mobster | Smooth talker, manipulative | Regain Evie, control the local crime scene |

---

### 20‑Chapter Outline  

> *Each chapter title hints at the emotional or narrative beat. Key scenes are marked with a brief dialogue snippet or descriptive touch.*

---

#### Chapter 1 – “The Last Pint”  
- Evie’s shop, **The Velvet Lantern**, is bustling.  
- Marco Borelli’s men demand Evie sells.  
- Evie refuses.  
> **Evie:** “You can’t buy this place with a bullet.”  
> *Description:* The neon sign flickers as a gunshot echoes in the alley behind.

#### Chapter 2 – “Blood on the Counter”  
- Luca watches from a distance, intrigued.  
- He enters, offering a “settlement” in a private booth.  
- Evie’s heart races.  
> **Luca:** “You’re the one who says no. Maybe you’re the one we need.”  

#### Chapter 3 – “Sins of the Past”  
- Luca reveals his mother’s murder.  
- Evie’s empathy grows; Luca’s façade cracks.  
> **Luca:** “Every night I hear her voice. I can’t live without that echo.”  
> *Description:* Candlelight shadows Luca’s face, mirroring the darkness in his eyes.

#### Chapter 4 – “The Family Code”  
- Luca introduces Evie to Sal and Marta.  
- The trio’s banter shows loyalty.  
> **Sal:** “You’ll be part of our family if you can survive the first night.”  
> *Description:* Sal’s hand trembles only slightly when he wipes the blood from his palm.

#### Chapter 5 – “Whispers in the Dark”  
- Detective Mara Quinn visits The Velvet Lantern to gather intel.  
- Evie suspects a trap.  
> **Mara:** “I hear whispers about the Borelli’s next move. Anything you’ve got would be useful.”  

#### Chapter 6 – “The First Kiss”  
- Luca saves Evie from a gang ambush.  
- Their hands touch; sparks fly.  
> **Luca (breathing heavily):** “You’re not just a bartender. You’re a shield.”  
> *Description:* The scent of rain mingles with the perfume of the night.

#### Chapter 7 – “Crossing Lines”  
- Evie learns about the Borelli’s illegal gambling rings.  
- She is torn: protect her shop or help them.  
> **Evie:** “I can’t let them bleed the city. Not while I’m still alive.”  

#### Chapter 8 – “The Deal”  
- Luca’s men offer Evie a partnership in exchange for silence.  
- She agrees, but only on her own terms.  
> **Luca:** “We’re not just a crime family. We’re a family.”  

#### Chapter 9 – “Fire in the Shadows”  
- Gino Santoro tries to usurp Marco’s control.  
- Luca intervenes, causing a shootout.  
> **Gino (shouting):** “You think you’re the only one who can hurt me?”  

#### Chapter 10 – “The Ties That Bind”  
- Evie and Luca’s relationship deepens, but the danger escalates.  
- Mara Quinn threatens to expose Luca.  
> **Mara:** “If you’re not careful, the law will swallow you.”  

#### Chapter 11 – “Luca’s Lament”  
- Luca confesses his trauma to Evie.  
- Evie offers her support.  
> **Luca:** “I keep my heart locked away. Maybe you’re the key.”  
> *Description:* Luca’s voice cracks, revealing vulnerability.

#### Chapter 12 – “The Night of Blood”  
- A rival gang ambushes the Borelli’s safe house.  
- Evie and Luca fight side‑by‑side.  
> **Evie (screaming):** “We can’t keep letting them win!”  
> *Description:* Blood stains the floor, echoing the stakes.

#### Chapter 13 – “A Dangerous Pact”  
- Luca’s brother Marco tries to make a deal with Gino.  
- Evie learns of the betrayal.  
> **Marco:** “We’ll split the empire, and you’ll just keep the bar.”  

#### Chapter 14 – “Love on the Edge”  
- Luca and Evie’s romance turns fiery.  
- They share a clandestine kiss in the back room of The Velvet Lantern.  
> **Evie:** “I never thought I’d find love in a world like this.”  
> *Description:* The flicker of a single candle lights the scene, casting long shadows.

#### Chapter 15 – “The Price of Power”  
- The police raid The Velvet Lantern.  
- Evie and Luca escape together.  
> **Mara:** “You’ll pay for the next time you cross me.”  

#### Chapter 16 – “The Family War”  
- Marco’s betrayal is exposed.  
- Luca and Evie must rally allies.  
> **Luca:** “We’re not just fighting a man. We’re fighting a system.”  

#### Chapter 17 – “The Heart of the Storm”  
- Evie’s family is threatened; she must choose between loyalty and love.  
- She sacrifices her own safety to protect Elena.  
> **Evie:** “I won’t let you become another casualty.”  

#### Chapter 18 – “Revenge and Redemption”  
- Luca confronts Gino in a final showdown.  
- Luca’s mother’s ghost appears as a metaphorical guide.  
> **Luca (whispering):** “I will make you proud, mother.”  

#### Chapter 19 – “The Crimson Vow”  
- After the battle, Luca and Evie make a promise to each other and the family.  
> **Evie:** “I’ll love you as fiercely as I protect my shop.”  
> *Description:* The sun rises over the city, painting the skyline crimson.

#### Chapter 20 – “New Beginnings”  
- Luca takes control of the Borelli family’s legitimate businesses.  
- Evie opens a new, safer location for The Velvet Lantern.  
- They look forward to a life of love and stability, but the underworld still watches.  
> **Luca:** “We’re not the same men we were before. But we’re stronger.”  

---

**Word Count Estimate:** 90 000 words  
- Average 4,500 words per chapter (including dialogue, description, and internal monologue).  

**Tone & Style:**  
- Rich, descriptive prose that paints the gritty streets of the city, the opulent underworld, and the intimate moments of romance.  
- Dialogue sharp, reflecting the tension of mafia politics and the tenderness of love.  
- Spicy romance interwoven with action; sensuality is conveyed through touch, gaze, and whispered promises rather than explicit scenes.  

**Key Themes:**  
- Family loyalty vs. personal desire.  
- The moral grayness of protecting loved ones in a corrupt world.  
- Healing and vulnerability in the face of trauma.  

**End Note:**  
*“Crimson Vows”* delivers a passionate, adrenaline‑filled romance that keeps readers on the edge of their seats while exploring the complex interplay between love, power, and survival in the dark heart of the mafia.



CHAPTER 1



The night had left the city in a bruised, half‑shadowed glow. Luca’s office, once a shrine of brass and mahogany, lay in ruin: a shattered glass chandelier hanging from the ceiling like a broken star, a pool of rainwater spreading across the polished floor, and a single, crimson‑stained envelope on the mahogany desk. Evie stood in the doorway, her hands wrapped around her own blood‑stained fingers, eyes flashing with the flicker of the neon sign behind the office.

“You’re still breathing,” Luca said, his voice low, the timbre of a man who had just survived a gun‑fire duel and a fight for his family. He stood in front of her, the shadows swallowing his shoulders. He pressed a hand over his chest, feeling the pounding heart that had just beaten the rhythm of the city’s violence. “I’m alive because of you.”

Evie swallowed. She’d spent the last twelve hours watching the blood spill out of the doors, the screams, the cold bite of the night. She had fought for her shop, her family, and for the man who had dared to love her in a world that would not let him. The air was thick with the smell of gunpowder and fear, but it was also the scent of the city’s broken heart, a scent that still clung to the corners of the room.

She stepped closer, her boots echoing on the broken floor tiles. “I’ll love you as fiercely as I protect my shop,” she said, her voice steadier now. “I promise you, Luca. We’ll keep each other alive.”

His eyes softened, the lines at his mouth forming a smile that was almost a laugh. “We’ll keep each other alive,” he repeated. “We’ll be stronger than the men who tried to take us down.” He reached out, his fingers brushing her cheek. The warmth of his skin against hers made the cold night feel like a promise.

They kissed there, in the ruined office, the world outside their walls fading into the distance. The kiss was urgent, desperate, an acknowledgment of the danger that still lurked beyond the office door. Luca's fingers were rough and strong, his palm pressing against her throat, while Evie’s hands were gripping his jaw, her nails digging into his skin as she pulled him closer. The smell of the city’s rainwater, the metallic tang of blood, and the faint scent of the coffee he had brewed in the kitchen all mingled in the air.

“Hold on,” Luca whispered, his lips brushing her ear. “We’re going to put this behind us, Evie. We’re going to rebuild.” He slid his hand into her hair, feeling the dampness of her hair that had been washed in the midnight rain. The texture of her hair was a reminder of how close she was to the outside world, how she had endured all of that and still survived. He felt a shiver run down his spine as his fingers tightened on her hair.

Evie’s pulse hammered against her ribs, a rhythm that matched the beat of his heart. She closed her eyes for a moment, and when she opened them again, they were shining with a fierce, unspoken promise. “You’re the only one who can keep me from losing myself,” she murmured, and the words seemed to echo around the room. “Let me be the one to keep you alive.” She pressed her lips to his once again, the kiss now deep, the taste of iron and rain on her tongue. She could feel the heat rising from his body, his skin slick with sweat from the fight. She could feel his breathing, ragged and fast, the sound a steady drumbeat in the quiet of the room. She could feel the weight of his hand on her waist, the way he had wrapped his arms around her, holding her close against the world.

Their bodies pressed together, and the air between them became a pressure point, a tangible force that seemed to fill the whole room. Luca's mouth pressed to her neck, his breath hot against her skin. He tasted the salt of her blood and the faint scent of coffee, a reminder that they had survived a battle that was as much about survival as it was about love. He kissed her again, a deep, passionate kiss that was a promise that they would fight and protect each other. The world outside was a distant blur, and all that mattered was the heat between them.

After a few minutes, they broke apart, their faces close together. Luca pressed his lips to her ear again, his breath carrying a message that was both a promise and a warning. “The city watches us, Evie. We’re not safe until we rebuild.”

Evie nodded. She looked up at him, her eyes shining with a fierce intensity that made his heart race. “We’ll rebuild, Luca. We’ll keep this place, this city, safe.” She leaned in for a kiss again, one that was fierce and bold, the taste of salt and sweat, the heat of his body pressed against her. The world outside faded, and all that remained was the two of them, a pair of lovers fighting for a future that would be better than the past.

The next day, as the sun rose over the city and painted the skyline crimson, Luca stood in front of the old warehouse that would become the new home of the Borelli family's legitimate businesses. Evie was already there, a stack of books in her arms, a smile on her face that made the city feel safe again. Luca stepped into the room, feeling the cool air against his skin, the dust that lingered from the old warehouse that still had the faint smell of oil and old wood. He looked at Evie, and she looked back with the fierce love that had made him stay in the city despite the danger.

“Luca,” she said, her voice soft but confident. “The new location is safer. We can keep the shop open, and we can keep you safe.”

“Thank you,” he said, taking her hand. His fingers were rough from the work, but he was holding her hand as if it were a fragile thing that could break at any moment. He smiled, a slow smile that showed how much she had done to keep him alive. “We’ll keep each other alive. I promise you.” He kissed her again, a kiss that was more tender than the last, the taste of salt on his lips, the scent of coffee still lingering on her skin.

The city outside seemed to breathe with them, and the sound of the waves that crashed on the harbor carried a rhythm that seemed to echo the beat of their hearts. As they stood together, the weight of the past, the fear and the promise of the future, all of it fell around them like a curtain. The city was alive, the air was full of hope, and Luca and Evie looked at each other with eyes that held a promise to keep each other alive. Their love was a fierce flame that burned against the dark world that still watched from the shadows. They held each other close, a promise that their future would be better than the past. Their love would keep them alive.

The door creaked open with the same low, reluctant sigh as the harbor at night, and the cold air that spilled into the room made Luca’s hair stand on end. He didn’t turn to look at the opening immediately; he stayed rooted to the spot where Evie had been leaning against the cracked plaster wall, her back to him, the faint outline of her shoulders illuminated by a single yellow bulb that sputtered like a dying ember.

The silhouette at the threshold was long, a shadowed figure that seemed to swallow the light. Luca's heart thudded in his chest, the memory of last week’s gunshot echoing in his ears. He felt the weight of the old wooden floorboards beneath his bare feet, the roughness of the dust under his fingers as he tried to steady himself.

“Don Salvatore?” Luca called, his voice a low whisper that barely carried over the murmur of waves from the harbor outside.

The figure stepped forward, the leather of his coat catching the dim light, and a faint scent of cologne—spicy, sharp, with an undertone of amber—wove through the air. Salvatore’s eyes were a stormy gray, and his lips were set in a hard line, like a man who had never been met by mercy. He was tall, broad-shouldered, and his presence filled the room like a stormcloud ready to break.

Evie turned, her eyes widening in a mix of shock and something that could have been fear or something else entirely. The curve of her jaw tightened; her lips moved to a thin line. She stared at the man, the one who had pulled her into this world and kept her alive, with a look that held both defiance and something raw and tender.

“You’re late, Luca.” The Don’s voice was smooth, but it carried the weight of an iron decree.

Luca stepped forward, the wood creaking under his boots. His hand tightened on the edge of the counter, knuckles whitening as he tried to keep his composure. “We’re not staying in this warehouse anymore,” he said, the words rough from the strain of the day, “We’re going to a new place, a new shop. It’s safer.”

Salvatore chuckled, a low, metallic sound that sounded like a gun being cocked. “Safer? Luca, you always think that the safest place is a place that’s empty. That’s a trap. That’s where I’ve buried a lot of men in the past.” He stepped closer, the distance between them shrinking to a dangerous inch. “You have no idea what they’re willing to do to keep a promise.”

Evie’s hand trembled as it reached for the small, cheap ring on her finger. The metal was cold against her skin. She looked back at Luca, seeing his face, the scar that ran along his jaw from a previous fight, his eyes darkened with anger and something that felt like love. The love that had kept him alive. He leaned in, his breath mingling with her, his lips brushing against her ear.

“Don,” Luca said, the words tasting like salt on his tongue, “she’s my life, Evie’s my heart. She’s the only thing that matters. Don’t you want to keep her?”

The Don’s eyes flickered for a moment, as if a secret had just crossed his mind. He paused, letting the silence hang between them like a knife. The only sound in the room was the hiss of a candle flame that burned in the corner, casting a dim amber glow across the walls.

“Don’t get sentimental.” The Don’s voice was sharp. He stepped close enough for Luca to smell the faint trace of gunpowder in the air. “You want her, but you don’t understand what I’ve built. The mafia doesn’t take a step back from its enemies. I’m not going to let you two run away from me. I’m going to take what I want.”

Luca’s hand clenched on the counter, the rough wood grain feeling like a promise of strength. “You’ll leave me alone, and I’ll keep Evie safe.” He swallowed, the taste of metal in his mouth.

Evie swallowed too. She could feel the Don’s pulse against her throat, his breath hot and cold. Her chest swayed, the faint tremor in her hand as she tried to keep her composure. She stepped forward, a step that felt like a leap of faith, her eyes never leaving Luca’s face. She pressed herself close to him, and in the space between them, a shiver of electricity ran through the air.

“Don Salvatore,” she said, her voice steady but her body trembling, “I want you to understand that you’re not the only one who can keep us safe. Luca will stand by my side, and if you’re not willing to accept that, you’re in no position to say you’re my family.”

The Don’s shoulders stiffened. He leaned back against the counter, and a small, almost imperceptible grin broke his grim demeanor. “You’re a stubborn little girl, Evie. But I see that Luca’s stubbornness has a… certain… allure.”

He stepped closer, his body filling the space between them, his hand resting on Evie’s waist, pulling her tighter to his chest. The heat that rose from the Don’s body was a tangible wave that washed over Evie’s skin, and she could feel the muscle under his fingers flex as he tightened his grip. The scent of his cologne clung to her like an intimate memory, an invitation.

“Don, please,” Luca’s voice cracked, but it held an iron edge. “Evie’s a woman, not a weapon. She’s not a tool to be used.”

The Don laughed, a sound that made the hairs on the back of Luca’s neck stand up. “You have a good heart, Luca. You have a good soul. I’ll give you one last chance.” He leaned in, his lips just a breath away from Evie’s ear, the heat from his body seeping through her clothes. “Don’t you want to see what happens when I touch you?”

Luca’s jaw clenched so hard that his teeth knocked together, a metallic sound that echoed in the narrow room. The Don’s hand was no longer on Evie’s waist but pressed against the small of her back, fingers tracing the line of her spine. Evie’s breath hitched; she could feel the Don’s lips brushing against the back of her neck, a tickle of fire that sent shivers down her spine.

“Don,” Luca whispered, his voice thick with rage and something deeper, a love that was both fierce and tender. “You’re the kind of man who wants to burn everything in his path. I won’t let you drag us into your darkness. Evie is a light in my darkness, and I won’t let you extinguish it.”

The Don’s eyes flicked to Luca, a mixture of admiration and menace in them. “Then we will see if the light can survive the darkness,” he said. He pulled his hand away from Evie’s back and lifted it to his mouth, his lips almost brushing the small, trembling ring on her finger.

Luca stepped forward, his hand reaching for Evie’s arm. Their fingers intertwined, a warm contact that sent a spark of electricity down Luca’s arms. Evie leaned into him, and the warmth of their bodies became a shield against the cold, looming threat that had entered their sanctuary.

In that cramped room, the waves outside seemed to roar louder, the harbor’s salt-slicked wind sweeping in, carrying with it the promise of an uncertain future. But for a fleeting moment, the only thing that mattered was the fierce, burning love that held them both together—an intimate promise that they would keep each other alive, even as the world outside threatened to tear them apart.

The Don’s jaw tightened, his knuckles whitening around the cheap gold chain that dangled from his collar. He took a slow, deliberate step back, letting the weight of his silhouette fill the room. The worn wooden floor creaked beneath the thud of his boots, a low percussion that seemed to echo the pounding in Evie’s chest. Luca’s shoulders were squared, the tension in his arms tightening like a rope that would snap with the slightest tug.

“You think you can play God with my world?” Luca’s voice was a low growl, reverberating off the brick walls. He lifted a hand, palm outstretched, the faint scent of gun oil and old whiskey clinging to his skin. “I’ve seen how deep your darkness goes, Luca. I know how your hands have stained the lives of those who crossed you.”

The Don’s eyes flickered to Luca, then to Evie. “You’re a child of the sea,” he said, the words a venomous promise. He took a step closer, closing the space between them. The room seemed to shrink as his presence pressed in, the air thickening with a metallic tang. He was the storm that had washed over Evie’s world, and he had the right to storm again.

“Don, stop,” Evie’s voice broke, a thin thread of fear braided into her plea. Her fingers tightened around Luca’s wrist, the small ring on her finger pulsing like a heart. “Luca, don’t—”

The Don’s hand slipped into the pocket of his jacket, a soft click as the zipper slid. He pressed his palm against Evie’s cheek, the skin cool against his warm, calloused palm. His fingertips left a faint, trembling trail down her jawline, the touch both tender and threatening. “You think I’ve only come to kiss you?” His voice was husky, almost a whisper that sent shivers along her spine. “I’ve come to claim what I am owed. And you, Evie, are the only thing that makes this debt feel…human.”

Luca’s jaw clenched. He lifted a hand, this time to shield Evie’s face from the Don’s penetrating stare. “You’re going to pay for this, Don. And you’re going to pay for every time you’ve crossed Evie’s line.”

The Don laughed, a dry, gravelly sound that cut through the silence like a blade. “Luca, you’re a man of principle and of blood. You know the game. I have a family, a territory, a legacy. And you, my little apprentice, are nothing but a pawn.”

The room seemed to tilt. Luca’s eyes narrowed. “Don, I’ve never wanted a life like yours. I only want to protect Evie. And that’s enough for me.”

The Don’s eyes softened for a heartbeat, a fleeting glimpse of the man behind the mask. He pulled his hand closer to Evie’s face, the faint scent of his cologne wrapping around her. He leaned in, his breath warm against her ear, the heat of his body against her cheek. “You’re a bright little star, Luca. A star that burns too fast for the night to hold. You’ll be a casualty of this war, if we’re not careful.”

Evie’s fingers tightened around Luca’s wrist, the ring on her finger glowing faintly in the dim light. She could feel the Don’s pulse, a steady thrum that rattled her chest. Her breath hitched, a soft inhale that felt like a secret. “Luca,” she whispered, her voice a tremulous plea. “Don, please—”

Luca stepped forward, the wooden floor beneath his feet echoing his resolve. He wrapped his arms around Evie, pulling her close as if he could anchor her to his body, to his heart. “Don, I won’t let you drag her into the storm.”

The Don’s lips curled into a predatory grin. He leaned in, the darkness of his eyes reflecting the dim glow of the single lantern. “You’re brave, little Luca. I admire that.”

He pressed his lips to Evie’s, the kiss a mixture of warmth and cold. The Don’s hand slid down her back, fingers tracing the curve of her spine with a mixture of reverence and menace. His mouth was soft and hungry, and Evie felt the heat of his breath as it brushed against her skin, a fire that set her hair aflame. Her pulse thumped in her ears. She could feel the Don’s pulse against her own, a rhythm that echoed in the quiet of the cramped room.

Luca’s grip tightened, his knuckles white, his breath shallow. “Don,” he said, voice thick with anger and something deeper—love, fear, an unbreakable bond. “I won’t let you drag us into your darkness. Evie is a light in my darkness, and I won’t let you extinguish it.”

The Don’s eyes flicked to Luca, a mixture of admiration and menace. “Then we will see if the light can survive the darkness,” he said. He pulled his hand away from Evie’s back and lifted it to his mouth, his lips almost brushing the small, trembling ring on her finger.

Luca stepped forward, his hand reaching for Evie’s arm. Their fingers intertwined, a warm contact that sent a spark of electricity down Luca’s arms. Evie leaned into him, and the warmth of their bodies became a shield against the cold, looming threat that had entered their sanctuary.

In that cramped room, the waves outside seemed to roar louder, the harbor’s salt‑slicked wind sweeping in, carrying with it the promise of an uncertain future. But for a fleeting moment, the only thing that mattered was the fierce, burning love that held them both together—an intimate promise that they would keep each other alive, even as the world outside threatened to tear them apart.

The Don’s eyes narrowed, a glint of something that looked almost like hunger. He leaned forward until his forehead pressed against the scarred wood of the doorframe, the same frame that had held their lives together and, in a way, their sins.

“Don,” Luca said, his voice low enough to be almost a hiss. “You’re playing with fire that isn’t yours to touch.”

The Don’s lips curled into a half‑smile that was more menace than amusement. “Fire? You, Luca, you think I’m a villain. You think I’m the one who’s been dragging Evie into a storm. I’ve seen the way she looks at me. I see the way she looks at you. I see the way the night falls over the harbor, and I know what she needs.”

He reached out, and his hand, rough as river stones, found the small, trembling ring on Evie’s finger. The silver caught the dim light from the single lamp, turning it into a tiny, desperate star. He lifted the ring to his lips, as if he could taste the truth it held, and closed his eyes. “Don,” he whispered, as if it were a confession, “I need her. I need her light to keep me alive in the night.”

Evie’s breath hitched. She could feel the heat from Luca’s body pressing against hers, the warmth of his sweat, the faint scent of his cologne, the way his pulse beat against her own chest. She had never seen the Don in this light, neither a villain nor a king. He was a man who could be a savior in one breath and a tyrant in the next. She could feel his hand on her back, the way it was both a promise and a threat.

The room was a dim rectangle of wood and old stone, the only source of light the flickering lamp. The walls were lined with faded portraits of men who had once called themselves “Don” and “Don.” The air smelled of old cigar smoke and the sea, the waves outside thundering like the drums of a thousand unseen enemies. Luca’s fingers trembled slightly, the metallic tang of sweat mixing with the brine on the walls. He could taste the fear, the promise of a night that might end in death or in a different kind of life.

Luca moved closer, his shoulder brushing against Evie’s thigh. She could feel the heat of his skin, the faint pulse that ran through him. He pressed his lips to the inside of her ear, his breath hot and smelling of the sea. “Don,” he said, his voice a low growl, “you’re going to break her heart, and you’ll have nothing left of yourself.”

The Don’s eyes flicked to Luca, a mixture of admiration and menace. He raised his hand to his mouth, his lips almost brushing the small, trembling ring on Evie’s finger. He could feel the pulse of his own heart, a drum in a place where love and hate could not coexist.

“Don,” Luca said again, but this time he was a different voice, softer but no less fierce. “I won’t let you drag us into your darkness. Evie is a light in my darkness, and I won’t let you extinguish it.”

The Don’s jaw clenched, his knuckles tightening on the ring. “Then we will see if the light can survive the darkness,” he said. The words were a promise, a threat, a lullaby for those who had lost everything. He turned back to Evie, and this time he did not pull his hand away. Instead, he pressed his forehead to hers, a small gesture of tenderness that felt like a prayer. He leaned forward and placed his lips on her neck, the taste of salt and sweat, the feel of her skin, the way her heart beat like a drum in the darkness.

Evie’s skin tingled, a shiver running up her spine. She could feel her pulse beating in the Don’s hands. She felt the heat of his breath against her ear, the taste of his saliva on her skin, the way he whispered words that sounded both like a vow and a threat. He leaned in again, his lips almost brushing her hair, as he whispered, “I’m yours, Evie. Don’t you see? I can’t imagine living a single breath without you.”

She could feel the Don’s breath on her skin, the taste of the sea on his lips, the way his voice made her feel both safe and terrified. She could feel Luca’s hand on her thigh, the way his pulse was a rhythm that echoed in her ears. She could feel the world outside, the roar of the waves, the salt‑slick wind that sweeps in, carrying with it the promise of an uncertain future. But in the cramped room, she could feel the heat of the Don’s breath, the softness of his lips, the way the world seemed to stop, and for a fleeting moment, the only thing that mattered was the fierce, burning love that held them both together.

The Don’s hand slipped to her shoulder, his fingers tracing a line along the curve of her spine. He lifted her chin with a gentle, but firm, touch. Evie looked at him, her eyes wide, filled with a mixture of fear, desire, and an understanding that she had never felt before. The Don’s lips brushed against her skin, the faint scent of his cologne drifting in the air. She could feel the heat of his breath on her skin, the softness of his lips against her neck. She could feel the pulse of his heart, the rhythm that echoed in her ears, the beat that made her feel alive, that made her feel like she was in a different world.

Luca stepped back, his arms wrapped around Evie, his fingers resting against her shoulder, the way a protector would do. “Don, we don’t have to do this,” he whispered, his voice barely audible above the sound of the waves. He could feel the tension in his own chest, the way his heart was beating in his ears, the way his breath was heavy, but he was determined to protect her.

The Don’s lips pulled away from Evie’s skin. His eyes were cold and cruel, a look that made the world go dark. He pressed his hands to his chest, his breath shallow, and looked at Luca. “I will not let you touch her.” He looked at Evie, his gaze softening, and then he said, “You should leave. The world is not ready for what we are. The world is not ready for a woman like me.” He turned to leave the room, but Luca was standing in the doorway.

The Don’s eyes flicked back to Luca, a mixture of admiration and menace. He raised his hand to his mouth, his lips almost brushing the small, trembling ring on Evie’s finger. He could feel the pulse of his own heart, a drum in a place where love and hate could not coexist. He turned back to Evie and leaned in again, this time his lips were close enough that he could taste her sweat. He whispered, “We will have a little game.” He then turned his attention to Luca and whispered, “I am going to do what I have to do.”

The Don’s hand slipped to Evie’s shoulder, his fingers tracing a line along the curve of her spine. He lifted her chin, his lips pressed to her neck, and he whispered, “I am going to do what I have to do.” He lifted her, and she could feel the heat of his breath on her skin, the softness of his lips on her neck. The world seemed to stop for a moment. The only thing that mattered was the fierce, burning love that held them both together.
